## 54. Codex

Time: `2026-07-04T09:04:23.334Z` · source line `70`

```text
Read-only inspection completed. No files were edited.

**PyTorch-Equivalent Forward**

The closest executable spec is [tools/stream1_transformer_torch_benchmark.py](D:/100XH100/.worktrees/stream1-piece-transformer/tools/stream1_transformer_torch_benchmark.py:42). For C++ LibTorch, reproduce this exported-weight forward:

1. Input `states`: `[B, 120]`, uint8/int, logical `State128.v[0..119]` only.
2. Build piece tokens:
   - `fast_piece_static`: `[50, 256]`
   - `fast_slot_projected`: `[3, 120, 256]`
   - `piece_positions`: `[50, 3]`
   - `piece_mask`: `[50, 3]`
   - For each slot, gather `states[:, piece_positions[:, slot]]`, add `fast_slot_projected[slot][value]` where mask is true.
   - Prepend `cls_token`: `[256]`, producing `x`: `[B, 51, 256]`.
3. `input_norm`: `LayerNorm(256, eps=1e-5)` over all 51 tokens.
4. Repeat 4 transformer blocks:
   - `y = LayerNorm(x, ln1, eps=1e-5)`
   - `qkv = y @ qkv_weight + qkv_bias`, shape `[B, 51, 768]`
   - reshape to Q/K/V `[B, 8, 51, 32]`
   - `scaled_dot_product_attention(q, k, v, dropout=0, causal=false)`
   - context back to `[B, 51, 256]`
   - `x = x + context @ attn_out_weight + attn_out_bias`
   - `y = LayerNorm(x, ln2, eps=1e-5)`
   - `y = SiLU(y @ ff1_weight + ff1_bias)`
   - `x = x + y @ ff2_weight + ff2_bias`
5. CLS pooling only:
   - `cls = LayerNorm(x[:, 0, :], output_norm, eps=1e-5)`
   - logits `[B, 24] = cls @ output_weight + output_bias`
6. Runtime score contract then clamps/rounds:
   - `round(clamp(logits, 0, 300) * 1024)`.

**Shapes / Manifest Contract**

From [tools/export_stream1_transformer.py](D:/100XH100/.worktrees/stream1-piece-transformer/tools/export_stream1_transformer.py:171) and [tools/stream1_weight_io.hpp](D:/100XH100/.worktrees/stream1-piece-transformer/tools/stream1_weight_io.hpp:345):

- `backend`: `piece_transformer`
- `dtype`: `fp16` or `bf16`
- `state_len`: `120`
- `num_classes`: `120`
- `move_count` / `output_dim`: `24`
- `num_pieces`: `50`
- `max_piece_size`: `3`
- `seq_len`: `51`
- `d_model`: `256`
- `nhead`: `8`
- `head_dim`: `32`
- `num_layers` / `transformer_layers`: `4`
- `ff_dim`: `1024`
- `activation`: `silu`
- `pooling`: `cls`
- `piece_layout`: `p900`
- `piece_embed_mode`: `full_s120`
- `input_embedding`: `fast_slot_projected`
- plus `move_names`, source paths, `model_id`, `model_name`.

Important exported file layout: weights named `*_hxk` are stored transposed for row-major C++ GEMM usage. The Torch benchmark loads e.g. `output_weight_hxk` as `[256, 24]`, while original PyTorch state dict has `output_layer.weight` as `[24, 256]`.

**Current CUDA Harness Notes**

[tools/stream_benchmark_transformer.cu](D:/100XH100/.worktrees/stream1-piece-transformer/tools/stream_benchmark_transformer.cu:65) benchmarks exported `piece_transformer` only, with sweep over `B_MICRO` and concurrency, optional CUDA graph mode via `BEAM_STREAM1_TRANSFORMER_GRAPH_BENCH`, and per-lane scratch. Kaggle notes use fp16 export on T4 and `BEAM_STREAM1_TRANSFORMER_BLOCK51=1`.

Latest inspected benchmark evidence shows native T4 fp16 around `~468k candidates/s` per T4 at good points, still below the PyTorch direct-forward reference `~735k candidates/s` on one T4. The gap analysis attributes that mainly to attention/kernel launch/layout behavior, not model mismatch.

**bf16 / fp16 / T4 / A100 Risks**

- T4 / SM75: use `fp16`. The CUDA path explicitly rejects bf16 attention/GEMM on SM75; bf16 requires SM80+.
- A100 / SM80: bf16 is supported by the current native path, but PyTorch-equivalent LibTorch should still validate numerics against reference JSON because bf16 rounding can shift quantized score keys.
- PyTorch SDPA selection is backend-dependent. A LibTorch implementation using `scaled_dot_product_attention` may pick different kernels across T4/A100 and torch/CUDA versions; for equivalence, compare quantized score keys, not raw logits only.
- Preserve `eps=1e-5`, pre-norm block order, CLS-only pooling, and SiLU. Any deviation there is a real model change.
- Do not reconstruct original local embeddings from checkpoint unless needed; the exported fast tables are already the folded inference contract.
```

```text
The closest executable spec is [tools/stream1_transformer_torch_benchmark.py](D:/100XH100/.worktrees/stream1-piece-transformer/tools/stream1_transformer_torch_benchmark.py:42). For C++ LibTorch, reproduce this exported-weight forward:

1. Input `states`: `[B, 120]`, uint8/int, logical `State128.v[0..119]` only.
2. Build piece tokens:

   - `piece_mask`: `[50, 3]`

   - Prepend `cls_token`: `[256]`, producing `x`: `[B, 51, 256]`.
3. `input_norm`: `LayerNorm(256, eps=1e-5)` over all 51 tokens.
4. Repeat 4 transformer blocks:
   - `y = LayerNorm(x, ln1, eps=1e-5)`
   - `qkv = y @ qkv_weight + qkv_bias`, shape `[B, 51, 768]`
   - reshape to Q/K/V `[B, 8, 51, 32]`
   - `scaled_dot_product_attention(q, k, v, dropout=0, causal=false)`
   - context back to `[B, 51, 256]`
   - `x = x + context @ attn_out_weight + attn_out_bias`
   - `y = LayerNorm(x, ln2, eps=1e-5)`
   - `y = SiLU(y @ ff1_weight + ff1_bias)`
   - `x = x + y @ ff2_weight + ff2_bias`
5. CLS pooling only:
   - `cls = LayerNorm(x[:, 0, :], output_norm, eps=1e-5)`
   - logits `[B, 24] = cls @ output_weight + output_bias`
6. Runtime score contract then clamps/rounds:
   - `round(clamp(logits, 0, 300) * 1024)`.
```
